# W1 UX pass - Architect answers (2026-10-16)

Decision: `D-2026-10-16-w1-ux`. Codex Architect, under `.ai/ROLE_CONFIG.md`. This answers the two questions in `tasks/W1-UX-mockup-fidelity.md` and governs W1-UX-01 through W1-UX-05. It amends the proposed disclosure-only pattern and approves a bounded vocabulary. Owner visual acceptance remains the combined W1 walkthrough.

## 1. Deeper analysis placement - replace the disclosure-only recommendation

The owner's T028 direction and `D-2026-10-04-plain-language-analysis-views` require a separate Analysis destination for substantive method, coverage, comparisons and calculation. Retain the existing Finding, Dispatch and Site Evidence `Overview | Analysis` pattern and the v69 contextual tab hierarchy. For other W1 detail roots, use an existing meaningful Analysis, Evidence, Plan, History, Verification or equivalent detail tab where it owns the deeper material. If a root has substantive technical explanation but no suitable destination, add one contextual **Analysis** tab in its owning W1 slice; do not add an empty tab or duplicate a record trail already owned by Evidence or History. The W1-UX-01 shared component may provide a `View analysis` link with selected Site, period, revision and object context; it must not make a closed disclosure the only route to substantive explanation.

Directory rows, index rows and compact Overview cards do not each need a tab strip. They link to the owning object's detail or Analysis destination. A closed inline **How this was worked out** disclosure is allowed for a few exact references, IDs, versions, raw reasons or support fields on a detail page. It is supplementary; a long table, causal explanation or evidence trail belongs in its contextual destination. Keep exact record links and historical selectors usable. Do not use a separate global expert mode.

At 1440 px, keep the contextual tabs visible under the page heading and link cards to the selected Analysis view. At 390 px, keep the **same named destination** as a keyboard-accessible tab or view link in the normal page flow, with wrapping or horizontal scrolling and an obvious active state; never replace it with a disclosure because the viewport is narrow. A small exact-reference disclosure may remain closed at either width. Material missing evidence, stale/pending/refused state, scope, and consequence limits stay beside the headline at both widths, outside any tab or disclosure. Native disclosure semantics and readable graphic text apply where a small disclosure is used.

## 2. Product vocabulary - accept with tighter boundaries

**Site, Evidence, Finding, Action and Verification** are approved domain labels for navigation and object identity. Explain their meaning in nearby ordinary language on first use in a view: a Finding is a bounded conclusion about an assessed period; an Action is proposed or recorded work; Evidence is the records used; Verification is a measured trial outcome when a result exists. **Analysis** is an approved destination label. Keep canonical route/tab names, including Verification, while a result card can say **Trial outcome**. A label grants no positive claim: `Finding` does not mean confirmed fault, `Action` does not mean applied, and `Verification` does not mean verified. Prefer **What the readings can support** over `claim limit`, **Reporting status** for source contact, and **Battery condition: cannot yet be judged** until a reviewed physical-condition read exists.

Default prose, headings, chart labels, badges, collapsed summaries and accessible names must not expose raw enums or reason codes (`CURRENT`, `UNASSESSED`, `VERIFIED`, etc.), UUIDs, digests, rule/model versions, cursor/watermark mechanics, internal analytics terms (`claim ceiling`, `claim limit`, `basis fingerprint`, `typed reason`) or simulator/development vocabulary (`run`, `binding`, `stage`, `commit`, `fixture`, `payload`) in its implementation sense. Ordinary operational language such as **the generator ran** is fine. Do not render a raw code in the collapsed control's accessible label. Source-owned or user-owned exact names may appear where identity itself is the subject, with a label and explanation; exact IDs and technical codes remain available in labelled fields in Analysis/Evidence or the small detail disclosure. No whole-screen or whole-response vocabulary exemption.

The ban on implementation language also applies inside Analysis and Evidence: technical depth explains the site's operation, evidence and limits, not AssetOps internals. Raw exact references are allowed there as labelled data for traceability. Authored example or generated test data must retain its explicit source qualification; it must not be presented as field equipment readings. W1-UX-01 should apply these rules to rendered API text, graphics, accessibility text and the proposed vocabulary scan.

## Claim order and implementation guard

**Honest state outranks presentation fidelity.** The selected, authorized read owns currentness, zero, amount, source, coverage, outcome and historical status. A headline, graphic or shorter label must not claim more than that read proves. Keep unavailable, refused, pending, stale, withdrawn, unassessed and proved-zero distinct. A fresh receipt is not fresh source facts or current analysis; source contact is not equipment condition; an applied Action is not a verified result; a modelled fuel-cost exposure is not realized loss or savings. Do not copy v69 illustrative numbers into product data. Put material qualifiers next to the answer even when the detailed basis has its own tab.

This is presentation direction only. W1 slices retain their source contracts, exact links, review gates and combined owner walkthrough. No backend or task-packet change is authorized by this decision.

## Findings

### 1. Medium (honest state) - W1-UX-01: example map collapses guardrail failure and overstates VERIFIED
`tasks/W1-UX-01-shared-presentation.md:9` maps `INEFFECTIVE` -> "The trial did not meet its goal" and `VERIFIED` -> "The measured trial met its goal and safety limits". Under `D-2026-10-16-s6-verification` (`.ai/DECISIONS.md:4155`) and `tasks/S6-02b-verification-ui.md:15`, `INEFFECTIVE` covers both a target miss and a measured reserve/service `GUARDRAIL_FAIL` (a reason, not an outcome), and S6-02b's accepted interim wording distinguishes "Target not met" from "Trial missed a safety or service limit" with metric/value/threshold/unit. A shared map keyed on the outcome enum alone would render a safety failure as an ordinary goal miss in every consumer. The VERIFIED sentence also drops two qualifiers the contract requires: the reserve guardrail is a "five-minute sampled guardrail, never continuous physical SOC proof" (`S6-02a-verification-core.md:15`), and only demo-source evidence may support a labelled demo verdict with "no savings or realized benefit". "Met its safety limits" is a stronger positive claim than the read proves.
Why it matters: the shared layer is what every later slice consumes; an error here propagates to Action, Verification, Recommendation and Changes.
Fix: key the outcome vocabulary on (outcome, reason) and source class, not the enum alone; keep the S6-02b guardrail-fail and target-miss sentences verbatim as the floor; VERIFIED wording must carry "sampled" guardrail scope and the demo/policy-trial label where the source is demo; add matrix cases for INEFFECTIVE+GUARDRAIL_FAIL, INEFFECTIVE target-only, INCONCLUSIVE with known failure, and demo-source VERIFIED.

### 2. Medium - W1-UX-01: scan extension breaks unmigrated screens; no ratchet plan
`W1-UX-01:13` extends `productVocabulary.json` with new terms (enum/reason-code shapes, "rule ID", "model version", raw IDs) and scans "every W1 product route", but phase 2 migrates only three cards. Existing tests assert zero violations on screens UX-02..05 will not touch until later (e.g. `frontend/src/shell/__tests__/s5SiteWindowOverview.test.tsx:217-218`, `frontend/src/sites/__tests__/siteAction.test.tsx:146-148`, and the other ~13 consumers of `scanProductVocabulary`). Adding terms globally either fails those tests on UX-01's branch, or pressures the implementer to exempt or weaken. The current scanner is also phrase-only (`frontend/src/test/productVocabulary.ts:57-64`), so "reason-code shapes" needs a new pattern mechanism the packet does not specify.
Fix: specify a ratchet. For example, UX-01 adds the new term families and pattern support behind a per-route opt-in (or a versioned "W1-UX" term set that each screen test adopts when its slice migrates it), proves the term set with mutation tests, and each of UX-02..05 turns it on for its own routes. Then the umbrella's final audit requires every W1 route to be on the full set before the combined walkthrough.

### 3. Medium (honest state) - W1-UX-01 and umbrella: limits can move into the closed disclosure, and the disclosure exemption is undefined
`W1-UX-01:7` puts "decision basis" into the closed "How this was worked out" disclosure. `W1-UX-01:13` scopes scan exemptions to "exact source-owned reference values and disclosure content". The current guard allows only the three exclusions in `D-2026-10-04-plain-language-analysis-views`, one single-value element at a time, and explicitly refuses exempting a container (`productVocabulary.ts:12-16`, `:116-121`). Exempting "disclosure content" wholesale reverses that rule, and it needs an Architect amendment of that decision. Together these let a stale/pending/limited/unknown explanation migrate into a collapsed region where nothing tests that it is still visible. "Show scope and limit near the headline" is stated but not tested.
Fix: (a) define the disclosure scan rule precisely: disclosure content is scanned against a narrower technical list, never skipped, and the single-value rule still holds for exemptions; route the change to D-2026-10-04 to the Architect. (b) Add a common acceptance test in UX-01 (and require it in each of UX-02..05): for every non-positive state (unassessed, pending, stale/non-current, unknown, refused, withheld money, proved-zero), the plain-language state sentence and its scope/as-of bound render outside the collapsed disclosure, with the disclosure closed.

### 4. Medium (honest state) - UX-02, UX-04, umbrella: watermark and cutoff moved to detail conflicts with proved-zero and as-of guarantees
`W1-UX-02:7` moves "cursor/watermarks" into `DigDeeper`, and the same packet keeps "S4-04 proved-zero watermark". Umbrella rows `sitefindings` and `sitework` (`W1-UX-mockup-fidelity.md:27,38`) say "cursor/watermark to detail". `W1-UX-04:9` moves "exact cutoff" to `DigDeeper`. A zero or current claim is only honest with its as-of bound visible; a "No open Findings" or "No active work" line without the watermark time reads as an unbounded positive.
Fix: state that the plain as-of time ("as of <time>") of any zero, current or historical selection stays in the default reading beside the claim. Only opaque cursor tokens and raw IDs move to detail. Cover this with the test in finding 3(b).

### 5. Medium - umbrella and UX-02: combined walkthrough is not mapped to screens, and the `sites` lead question overstates the read
(a) Walkthrough: `W1-UX-mockup-fidelity.md:54` gives an ordered tool list but no screen -> tool -> route map, so it cannot be checked that all 26 screens are reachable. It omits `tools/s405_provenance_walkthrough.py` (S4-05b, the walkthrough that triggered this pass, and whose "interim source labels" UX-03 revisits) and `tools/s502_overview_finding_list_walkthrough.py` (S5-02 Overview Finding list, a UX-02 surface). It names an unspecified "S1 owner fixture" where `tools/v01a_foundation_walkthrough.py` exists. No packet owns producing the ordered list before the owner session.
Fix: add a 26-row table (screen ID, tool, prepare/serve flags, route, which state it demonstrates: supported/limited/stale/proved-zero), add the S4-05b and S5-02 tools, name the Foundation tool, and assign authorship (for example, the UX-05 closeout or the coordinator) with an explicit gate before the walkthrough is scheduled.
(b) `sites` lead: umbrella row 15 and `W1-UX-02:7` use "Which site needs attention?", while the mock's question is "Which site should I open?" (`screen-manifest.json`, `sites`). "Needs attention" implies a per-site condition ranking the read cannot supply (`sites:2` and estate health are gaps), which contradicts the same row's "No estate health inference". Fix: use the mock question, or another phrasing that does not imply ranking.

### 6. Low - two inventory anchors point at refusal states, not the named seam
`SitesIndex.tsx:108-132` is the unavailable/empty panels; the inventory table starts at `:133` (the registry anchor is `:134`). `SiteFindingHistory.tsx:145-171` is the refusal-words map, not the history timeline. Fix: re-anchor. This is non-blocking because implementers re-inventory.

### 7. Low - existing per-screen word maps are not inventoried for consolidation
Word maps already exist in `dispatchWords.ts`, `findingWords.ts`, `findingPresentation.tsx:33,329`, `actionPresentation.tsx:21,53,64`, `EvidenceClaimLimit.tsx:39-128`, `SiteFinancials.tsx:43`, `FindingConsequence.tsx:34,63,400`, `OverviewFindingList.tsx:262`, and S6-02b adds `verificationWords.ts`. UX-01 asks for "one" map but does not say whether it absorbs, wraps or coexists with these. Without that, a parallel map is likely. Fix: list them and state the migration rule (shared map owns cross-screen states; screen maps keep domain-specific words and are read through it).

### 8. Low - layout proof tool and the not-run rule are not named
The packets require 1440/390 app-versus-mock screenshots but do not name the mechanism. A precedent exists (`MOCK = (id) => .../mockups/v69/index.html#/${id}` in `tools/v01a-foundation-evidence.mjs:137` and `tools/v01b-lab-evidence-evidence.mjs:222`). Fix: name the per-slice evidence script pattern, and require a skipped capture to be recorded as `not run` with its blocking precondition.

### 9. Low - baseline and effort ambiguity
`W1-UX-01:3` branches from "merged W1 UI baseline", but `I-W1-UX-01` requires only `A-S5-overview`. State whether UX-01 waits for S6-02b, S6-03 and S7-01b; otherwise verification and health vocabulary lands in UX-04/05, not the shared layer. UX-04 covers nine screens, including the 1420-line `SiteActionVerification.tsx`, so registry effort `M` is likely under-sized (consider `L`).

### 10. Low - `PENDING_REASSESSMENT` example wording implies active processing
"New readings are being checked" asserts that work is in progress; the state means a reassessment is due. The packet already requires entries to be reviewed against the typed source. Flagged only so the reviewer of UX-01 checks it.

## Residual risk not named in the packets

- UX-04 restyles S6-02b's guardrail and withdrawn-outcome presentation shortly after S6-02b merges. The fail-closed tests in `siteActionVerification.test.tsx` (549 lines on the branch) are the only guard; the UX-04 reviewer should diff that test file and treat any weakened assertion as blocking.
- Visual "headline plus visual" cards invite derived graphics (bars, timelines) whose geometry can imply a value the read did not give (for example a zero-length bar for unknown). UX-01's matrix should include an unknown-value visual case that renders as a distinct "not known" mark, not zero.

Use an ordered list of existing private walkthrough tools because their guarded DBs, state transitions and ports differ; do not blend authored fixtures into a synthetic live Site. Coordinator prepares and serves one case at a time with the tool's private DB/work prefix, records exact URL and dataset provenance, and drops it after evidence capture: `tools/s5_overview_walkthrough.py` (Overview, Finding, money), `tools/s404_finding_index_walkthrough.py`, `tools/s402b_fuel_cost_walkthrough.py` plus `tools/s402a_assumptions_walkthrough.py`, `tools/s302_claim_limit_walkthrough.py` plus `tools/s3_dispatch_walkthrough.py`, `tools/s601b_action_walkthrough.py`, then the merged S6-02b, S6-03 and S7-01b walkthrough tools named in their task packets. Add `tools/s2_demo_walkthrough.py` for accepted/rejected Evidence and the S1 owner fixture for Foundation/Readiness/SLD. An index page/script may print this ordered URL list, but it must not mutate datasets or claim a single common lineage. Capture one supported, one limited/pending, one stale/withdrawn and one proved-zero path where their read supports them; 1440 and 390 screenshots, keyboard disclosure, exact links/reload, no cross-case numeric comparisons. Owner walks the complete W1 journey once and records acceptance; W1 completes only then.

2. Recommend retaining approved domain words â€œFindingâ€, â€œActionâ€, â€œEvidenceâ€, â€œSiteâ€, â€œVerificationâ€ when explained by nearby plain text. # W1-UX-01 â€” shared product presentation

Status: implementation-ready. Branch `task/W1-UX-01` from merged W1 UI baseline. Frontend only. This slice precedes W1-UX-02â€“05. Read [umbrella](W1-UX-mockup-fidelity.md), owner notes named there, `frontend/mockups/v69/index.html`, screen manifest and current `frontend/src/test/productVocabulary.ts` / `.json`. USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED: one combined W1 walkthrough after all slices; independent slice review is the merge gate.

## Result and contract

Build one shared `ProductStatus`/`ProductLimitation` presentation map, `DigDeeper` disclosure and `HeadlineVisualCard` (names are suggestions) in `frontend/src/ui/`. Components accept semantic **already-read** state and explicit evidence, never derive currentness, outcome, amount, zero, completeness or source health. Use the mock's hierarchy: one answer headline, one small visual (timeline, comparison, status bar or causal steps), then â€œWhat this means / What to doâ€; show scope and limit near the headline. Empty/unknown remain visible first-class states. Accessible labels, textual graphic equivalents, native keyboard disclosure, 390 px no overflow. Exact record IDs, raw enums, rule IDs, model versions, digests and decision basis belong in a closed disclosure labelled â€œHow this was worked outâ€; exact source/user-owned names in the default reading are permitted only where their identity is the subject, never as unexplained prose. Keep existing URL selectors and source links.

Single vocabulary map (examples; final entries must be reviewed against each typed source, not guessed): `UNASSESSED` â†’ â€œWe have not checked this periodâ€; `PENDING_REASSESSMENT` â†’ â€œNew readings are being checkedâ€; `STALE`/upstream out of date â†’ â€œThis result may miss newer readingsâ€; `UNKNOWN`/not configured â†’ â€œWe cannot judge this yetâ€ / â€œThis part has not been set upâ€; `CURRENT` â†’ â€œUsing the latest checked readingsâ€ **only when the read proves it**; `INCONCLUSIVE` â†’ â€œWe could not tell whether the change workedâ€; `INEFFECTIVE` â†’ â€œThe trial did not meet its goalâ€; `VERIFIED` â†’ â€œThe measured trial met its goal and safety limitsâ€ with scope. Reason-code families map to a cause sentence plus next useful step: missing report, incomplete period, stale source, pending computation, incompatible comparison, missing configuration, refused read. Unknown codes fall back to neutral â€œWe cannot explain the limit from this recordâ€ plus closed technical code, never print the code in headline. Money has distinct â€œNo current amount can be shownâ€ and â€œNo price recordedâ€; no `$0` substitution. Read refusal differs from proved empty.

Extend `frontend/src/test/productVocabulary.ts` and `.json` from T028 to scan the default reading of every W1 product route, including rendered API text, SVG labels/titles, badges and accessibility attributes. Add terms for known leaked enums/reason-code shapes, â€œrule IDâ€, â€œmodel versionâ€, internal analytics phrases and raw IDs where they are authored prose. Scope exemptions narrowly to exact source-owned reference values and disclosure content, retaining the current single-value exemption rule. Do not exempt a whole screen, card or response. Keep a fixture matrix showing one populated, one missing and one refused state per presentation family; assert the scan saw nonzero text and rejects deliberate leaked terms outside detail. Inspect the existing `Advanced`/`Disclosure` primitives before adding a new one; adapt or wrap instead of duplicating behavior.

+from pathlib import Path
+
+root = Path('tasks')
+
+def edit(name, changes=(), inserts=()):
+    path = root / name
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    for old, new in changes:
+        if old not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing {old[:80]!r}')
+        s = s.replace(old, new)
+    for marker, addition in inserts:
+        if marker not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing marker {marker!r}')
+        s = s.replace(marker, addition + '\n\n' + marker, 1)
+    path.write_text(s, encoding='utf-8')

+from pathlib import Path
+
+root = Path('tasks')
+
+def edit(name, changes=(), inserts=()):
+    path = root / name
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    for old, new in changes:
+        if old not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing {old[:80]!r}')
+        s = s.replace(old, new)
+    for marker, addition in inserts:
+        if marker not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing marker {marker!r}')
+        s = s.replace(marker, addition + '\n\n' + marker, 1)
+    path.write_text(s, encoding='utf-8')
+

+from pathlib import Path
+
+root = Path('tasks')
+
+def edit(name, changes=(), inserts=()):
+    path = root / name
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    for old, new in changes:
+        if old not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing {old[:80]!r}')
+        s = s.replace(old, new)
+    for marker, addition in inserts:
+        if marker not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing marker {marker!r}')
+        s = s.replace(marker, addition + '\n\n' + marker, 1)
+    path.write_text(s, encoding='utf-8')
+
+decision = ('## Architect questions', '## Architect ruling\n\nQuestions answered by `D-2026-10-16-w1-ux` in `.agent/architect-W1-ux-answers.md`. Its destination, vocabulary and honest-state boundaries govern this packet.')
+
+name = 'W1-UX-01-shared-presentation.md'
+s = (root / name).read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+s = s.replace('from merged W1 UI baseline', 'after S6-02b, S6-03 and S7-01b merge, from that merged W1 UI baseline')
+start = s.index('Build one shared ')
+end = s.index('\n\nSingle vocabulary map', start)

/**
 * Where the operator shell puts its site surfaces.
 *
 * One module owns the operator site addresses, so the route table, the
 * navigation list, the Site tab row, and the link out of a Sites index row
 * cannot disagree about where a site lives. These are operator paths, not
 * simulator paths: they are served in both gate states and nothing here reads
 * the feature flag.
 *
 * A site is addressed by `site_id` and by nothing else. There is no
 * parameterless site destination here at all. The T002 `/site-details` and
 * `/site-configuration` frames were route placeholders from before site
 * identity existed, and a link that names no site is not a destination. Each
 * was removed by the slice that made its identified route real: Site Details
 * by T007, the Foundation surface by T008. Both are reached from a site, and
 * neither is an operator navigation item, because a navigation item cannot say
 * which site it would open.
 *
 * A site's foundation hangs off that site's address rather than sitting beside
 * it. One site is one subject, and its foundation is an aspect of it, so the
 * address says so.
 *
 * The product name of that surface is Foundation, from v6.9's operator Site
 * tab row and its Foundation content row. T008 built it under the working name
 * Site Configuration and T011A renames it. `configuration` remains the domain
 * word for the document a site is created from - a site's configuration origin
 * is still configuration origin, and configuration is still fixed at creation -
 * but the screen the operator opens is Foundation.
 */

/** The operator Sites index. Not a simulator path, and never gated. */
export const SITES_PATH = "/sites";

/** The route pattern for one site's page. */
export const SITE_DETAIL_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITES_PATH}/:siteId`;

/**
 * The address of one site's page.
 *
 * The identity is encoded rather than interpolated raw. A `site_id` is
 * charset-constrained at the backend port, so nothing awkward can reach this
 * for a configured site; encoding is what keeps a *requested* identity that is
 * not a valid one from changing the shape of the address it is refused at.
 */
export function siteDetailHref(siteId: string): string {
  return `${SITES_PATH}/${encodeURIComponent(siteId)}`;
}

/** The route pattern for one site's Foundation. */
export const SITE_FOUNDATION_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITE_DETAIL_ROUTE_PATH}/foundation`;

/**
 * The address of one site's Foundation.
 *
 * Built from the site's own address, for the same reason it is encoded there:
 * the two can then never disagree about how a site is spelled into a URL.
 */
export function siteFoundationHref(siteId: string): string {
  return `${siteDetailHref(siteId)}/foundation`;
}

/**
 * S6-01b: one Foundation change by its stable change ID, a deep route under
 * Foundation that is not a Foundation view (`FOUNDATION_SUBTABS` never lists
 * it). The bare `/foundation/changes` address has its own explicit
 * unavailable state: there is no Site change index in S6-01. Both are static
 * segments, so they rank above `:foundationView` and never reach the view frame.
 */
export const SITE_FOUNDATION_CHANGES_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITE_DETAIL_ROUTE_PATH}/foundation/changes`;
export const SITE_FOUNDATION_CHANGE_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITE_FOUNDATION_CHANGES_ROUTE_PATH}/:changeId`;

export function siteFoundationChangeHref(siteId: string, changeId: string): string {
  return `${siteFoundationHref(siteId)}/changes/${encodeURIComponent(changeId)}`;
}

/**
 * The route pattern for one of the Foundation's other views (S1-03): the
 * Single line diagram (`sld`) and configuration Readiness (`readiness`). The
 * Site model is the Foundation address itself. Which segments are views is
 * owned by `FOUNDATION_SUBTABS`; any other segment is not a view and is
 * refused by the frame, never rendered as a disabled view.
 */
export const SITE_FOUNDATION_VIEW_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITE_DETAIL_ROUTE_PATH}/foundation/:foundationView`;

/**
 * The address of one Foundation view, carrying the selected period and, when
 * one is chosen, the selected part by its stable component ID (V-01a round 2,
 * `D-2026-10-12-v01a-owner-visual-rulings`). The two are independent.
 */
export function siteFoundationViewHref(
  siteId: string,
  segment: string,
  at: string | null,
  component: string | null = null,
): string {
  const base = segment === "" ? siteFoundationHref(siteId) : `${siteFoundationHref(siteId)}/${segment}`;
  const query = new URLSearchParams();
  if (at !== null) query.set("at", at);
  if (component !== null) query.set(FOUNDATION_COMPONENT_PARAM, component);
  const search = query.toString();
  return search === "" ? base : `${base}?${search}`;
}

/** The query key that carries the selected part's component ID. */
export const FOUNDATION_COMPONENT_PARAM = "component";

/** The route pattern for one site's Evidence. */
export const SITE_EVIDENCE_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITE_DETAIL_ROUTE_PATH}/evidence`;

/** The address of one site's Evidence, built from the site's own address. */
export function siteEvidenceHref(siteId: string): string {
  return `${siteDetailHref(siteId)}/evidence`;
}

/**
 * The Site > Evidence record inspector for one accepted record (T027 phase 2:
 * Overview evidence links). `signal` is `DEVICE/SIGNAL`, the same value the
 * inspector reads from its own signal rows, so the root does not change.
 */
/** Site Evidence > Analysis with one assessed period's record coverage selected. */
export function siteEvidenceAssessmentHref(siteId: string, assessmentId: string): string {
  return `${siteEvidenceHref(siteId)}?view=analysis&assessment=${encodeURIComponent(assessmentId)}`;
}

export function siteEvidenceRecordHref(
  siteId: string,
  signal: string,
  messageId: string,
): string {
  const [device, ...rest] = signal.split("/");
  const signalParam = `${encodeURIComponent(device)}/${encodeURIComponent(rest.join("/"))}`;
  return `${siteEvidenceHref(siteId)}?signal=${signalParam}&record=${encodeURIComponent(messageId)}`;
}

/** The route patterns for one site's Performance (T028), and one subtab of it. */
export const SITE_PERFORMANCE_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITE_DETAIL_ROUTE_PATH}/performance`;
export const SITE_PERFORMANCE_SUBTAB_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITE_PERFORMANCE_ROUTE_PATH}/:capabilityId`;

/**
 * The address of one site's Performance, optionally at one subtab. A subtab is
 * a backend capability id (`dispatch`), never a profile label.
 */
export function sitePerformanceHref(siteId: string, capabilityId?: string): string {
  const base = `${siteDetailHref(siteId)}/performance`;
  return capabilityId === undefined ? base : `${base}/${encodeURIComponent(capabilityId)}`;
}

/** The route pattern for one site's scoped Findings index (T028). */
export const SITE_FINDINGS_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITE_DETAIL_ROUTE_PATH}/findings`;

export function siteFindingsHref(siteId: string): string {
  return `${siteDetailHref(siteId)}/findings`;
}

/**
 * S4-01: one durable Finding of a Site (`A-claim-work-routes`). An exact
 * version is `?revision_id=`, the same parameter the API takes, so a reload
 * reopens that stored version.
 */
export const SITE_FINDING_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITE_FINDINGS_ROUTE_PATH}/:findingId`;

+from pathlib import Path
+
+root = Path('tasks')
+
+def edit(name, changes=(), inserts=()):
+    path = root / name
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    for old, new in changes:
+        if old not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing {old[:80]!r}')
+        s = s.replace(old, new)
+    for marker, addition in inserts:
+        if marker not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing marker {marker!r}')
+        s = s.replace(marker, addition + '\n\n' + marker, 1)
+    path.write_text(s, encoding='utf-8')
+

export function siteFindingHref(siteId: string, findingId: string, revisionId?: string | null): string {
  const base = `${siteFindingsHref(siteId)}/${encodeURIComponent(findingId)}`;
  return revisionId ? `${base}?revision_id=${encodeURIComponent(revisionId)}` : base;
}

/**
 * S4-02a: Site > Financials. The Overview optionally carries a Finding bridge
 * (`?finding_id=&revision_id=`, the exact FindingRevision); Assumptions takes
 * an exact price version (`?revision_id=`) or a recorded cutoff
 * (`?recorded_at=`), the same parameters the read API takes, so reload and
 * back reopen the same stored selection.
 */
export const SITE_FINANCIALS_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITE_DETAIL_ROUTE_PATH}/financials`;
export const SITE_FINANCIAL_ASSUMPTIONS_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITE_FINANCIALS_ROUTE_PATH}/assumptions`;

export function siteFinancialsHref(
  siteId: string,
  bridge?: { findingId: string; revisionId?: string | null; consequenceRevisionId?: string | null },
): string {
  const base = `${siteDetailHref(siteId)}/financials`;
  if (!bridge) return base;
  const query = new URLSearchParams({ finding_id: bridge.findingId });
  if (bridge.revisionId) query.set("revision_id", bridge.revisionId);
  if (bridge.consequenceRevisionId) query.set("consequence_revision_id", bridge.consequenceRevisionId);
  return `${base}?${query.toString()}`;
}

export function siteFinancialAssumptionsHref(
  siteId: string,
  pin?: { revisionId?: string | null; recordedAt?: string | null },
): string {
  const base = `${siteDetailHref(siteId)}/financials/assumptions`;
  const query = new URLSearchParams();
  if (pin?.revisionId) query.set("revision_id", pin.revisionId);
  if (pin?.recordedAt) query.set("recorded_at", pin.recordedAt);
  const text = query.toString();
  return text ? `${base}?${text}` : base;
}

/**
 * S4-03: the named child views of the canonical Finding root. Each is
 * `/sites/{site}/findings/{finding}/{view}[?revision_id=]` and keeps the same
 * exact-version parameter. The frame's switch (`SiteFindingFrame`) renders
 * each view from this list (Evidence: phase 2, History: phase 3).
 * S6-01b adds Recommendation (`recommendation:1`), which keeps the same
 * `?revision_id=` so the recommendation is always for an exact version.
 */
export const FINDING_CHILD_VIEWS = ["evidence", "history", "recommendation"] as const;
export type FindingChildView = (typeof FINDING_CHILD_VIEWS)[number];
export const SITE_FINDING_VIEW_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITE_FINDING_ROUTE_PATH}/:findingView`;

export function siteFindingViewHref(
  siteId: string,
  findingId: string,
  view: FindingChildView,
  revisionId?: string | null,
): string {
  const base = `${siteFindingsHref(siteId)}/${encodeURIComponent(findingId)}/${view}`;
  return revisionId ? `${base}?revision_id=${encodeURIComponent(revisionId)}` : base;
}

/**
 * S6-01b: the canonical Site Action root and its child sections. An exact
 * event is `?event_id=`, a server recorded cutoff is `?recorded_at=`, the same
 * parameters the read API takes; the selection is carried into every section
 * so reload and navigation keep it. The `execution` segment is the address
 * only; its label is "Application".
 */
export const SITE_ACTION_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITE_DETAIL_ROUTE_PATH}/actions/:actionId`;
export const SITE_ACTION_VIEW_ROUTE_PATH = `${SITE_ACTION_ROUTE_PATH}/:actionView`;
export const ACTION_CHILD_VIEWS = ["execution", "history"] as const;
export type ActionChildView = (typeof ACTION_CHILD_VIEWS)[number];

export function siteActionHref(
  siteId: string,
  actionId: string,
  view: ActionChildView | "overview" = "overview",
  selector?: { eventId?: string | null; recordedAt?: string | null },
): string {
  const root = `${siteDetailHref(siteId)}/actions/${encodeURIComponent(actionId)}`;
  const base = view === "overview" ? root : `${root}/${view}`;
  const query = new URLSearchParams();
  if (selector?.eventId) query.set("event_id", selector.eventId);
  if (selector?.recordedAt) query.set("recorded_at", selector.recordedAt);
  const text = query.toString();
  return text ? `${base}?${text}` : base;
}

/**
 * The Site Evidence record inspector opened by one exact accepted evidence ID
 * (the central-ledger inspector reads `record=` as that ID in Analysis). Never
 * a message ID: a message ID can name more than one stored record.
 */
export function siteEvidenceAcceptedRecordHref(siteId: string, evidenceId: string): string {
  return `${siteEvidenceHref(siteId)}?view=analysis&record=${encodeURIComponent(evidenceId)}`;
}

/**
 * The Findings rail root (T028): the global index. A Finding is a first-class
 * object under this root, so opening one from a Site changes the root to the
 * Finding; its claim drawer is a query parameter and never changes it.
 */
export const FINDINGS_PATH = "/findings";
export const FINDING_ROUTE_PATH = `${FINDINGS_PATH}/:siteId/:assessmentId`;
export const FINDING_TAB_ROUTE_PATH = `${FINDING_ROUTE_PATH}/:findingTab`;

/**
 * Finding detail tabs rendered at T028 (D-2026-10-04-plain-language-analysis-
 * views adds Analysis); Recommendation and History land at T034.
 */
export type FindingTab = "overview" | "analysis" | "evidence" | "alternatives";

export function findingHref(siteId: string, assessmentId: string, tab: FindingTab = "overview"): string {
  const base = `${FINDINGS_PATH}/${encodeURIComponent(siteId)}/${encodeURIComponent(assessmentId)}`;

+from pathlib import Path
+
+root = Path('tasks')
+
+def edit(name, changes=(), inserts=()):
+    path = root / name
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    for old, new in changes:
+        if old not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing {old[:80]!r}')
+        s = s.replace(old, new)
+    for marker, addition in inserts:
+        if marker not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing marker {marker!r}')
+        s = s.replace(marker, addition + '\n\n' + marker, 1)
+    path.write_text(s, encoding='utf-8')
+

Analysis is an approved destination. These labels do not imply a confirmed fault, applied work or verified result. +Example map entries must be checked against typed reads: `UNASSESSED` → “We have not checked this period”; `PENDING_REASSESSMENT` → “New readings call for another assessment” (no claim that processing has started); `STALE` → “This result may miss newer readings”; `UNKNOWN` → “We cannot judge this yet”; `CURRENT` → “Using the latest checked readings” only when proved; `INCONCLUSIVE` → “Could not conclude”, with any known failure still visible; `INEFFECTIVE` plus measured reserve/service `GUARDRAIL_FAIL` → **“Trial missed a safety or service limit”**, with failed metric, value, threshold and unit beside it; target-only `INEFFECTIVE` → **“Target not met”**. Key outcome wording on **(outcome, reason, source class)**, not outcome alone. Demo-source outcomes visibly say **policy trial** and **no savings or realized benefit**. Keep S6-02b's guardrail-fail, target-miss and unknown-fact sentences as the wording floor. Reason families get a cause sentence and next useful step. Unknown codes get neutral “We cannot explain the limit from this record”, with labelled exact code only in detail. Money distinguishes “No current amount can be shown” from “No price recorded”; never substitute `$0`. Refusal differs from proved empty. Authored examples, generated tests and simulator outputs retain explicit demonstration/simulated source qualification and never appear as field readings.''' + s[end:]

+from pathlib import Path
+
+root = Path('tasks')
+
+def edit(name, changes=(), inserts=()):
+    path = root / name
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    for old, new in changes:
+        if old not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing {old[:80]!r}')
+        s = s.replace(old, new)
+    for marker, addition in inserts:
+        if marker not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing marker {marker!r}')
+        s = s.replace(marker, addition + '\n\n' + marker, 1)
+    path.write_text(s, encoding='utf-8')
+

+
+root = Path('tasks')

+def edit(name, changes=(), inserts=()):
+    path = root / name
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    for old, new in changes:
+        if old not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing {old[:80]!r}')
+        s = s.replace(old, new)
+    for marker, addition in inserts:
+        if marker not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing marker {marker!r}')
+        s = s.replace(marker, addition + '\n\n' + marker, 1)
+    path.write_text(s, encoding='utf-8')

+from pathlib import Path
+
+root = Path('tasks')
+
+def edit(name, changes=(), inserts=()):
+    path = root / name
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    for old, new in changes:
+        if old not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing {old[:80]!r}')
+        s = s.replace(old, new)
+    for marker, addition in inserts:
+        if marker not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing marker {marker!r}')
+        s = s.replace(marker, addition + '\n\n' + marker, 1)
+    path.write_text(s, encoding='utf-8')
+

+from pathlib import Path
+
+root = Path('tasks')
+
+def edit(name, changes=(), inserts=()):
+    path = root / name
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    for old, new in changes:
+        if old not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing {old[:80]!r}')
+        s = s.replace(old, new)
+    for marker, addition in inserts:
+        if marker not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing marker {marker!r}')
+        s = s.replace(marker, addition + '\n\n' + marker, 1)
+    path.write_text(s, encoding='utf-8')
+

+from pathlib import Path
+
+root = Path('tasks')
+
+def edit(name, changes=(), inserts=()):
+    path = root / name
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    for old, new in changes:
+        if old not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing {old[:80]!r}')
+        s = s.replace(old, new)
+    for marker, addition in inserts:
+        if marker not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing marker {marker!r}')
+        s = s.replace(marker, addition + '\n\n' + marker, 1)
+    path.write_text(s, encoding='utf-8')
+

Analysis is an approved destination. These labels do not imply a confirmed fault, applied work or verified result. Prefer “What the readings can support”, “Reporting status” and “Battery condition: cannot yet be judged”.
+
+Example map entries must be checked against typed reads: `UNASSESSED` → “We have not checked this period”; `PENDING_REASSESSMENT` → “New readings call for another assessment” (no claim that processing has started); `STALE` → “This result may miss newer readings”; `UNKNOWN` → “We cannot judge this yet”; `CURRENT` → “Using the latest checked readings” only when proved; `INCONCLUSIVE` → “Could not conclude”, with any known failure still visible; `INEFFECTIVE` plus measured reserve/service `GUARDRAIL_FAIL` → **“Trial missed a safety or service limit”**, with failed metric, value, threshold and unit beside it; target-only `INEFFECTIVE` → **“Target not met”**. Key outcome wording on **(outcome, reason, source class)**, not outcome alone. For `VERIFIED`, say the measured target was met within the **five-minute sampled** reserve/service guardrails; never imply continuous physical SOC proof. Demo-source outcomes visibly say **policy trial** and **no savings or realized benefit**. Keep S6-02b's guardrail-fail, target-miss and unknown-fact sentences as the wording floor. Reason families get a cause sentence and next useful step. Unknown codes get neutral “We cannot explain the limit from this record”, with labelled exact code only in detail. Money distinguishes “No current amount can be shown” from “No price recorded”; never substitute `$0`. Refusal differs from proved empty. Authored examples, generated tests and simulator outputs retain explicit demonstration/simulated source qualification and never appear as field readings.''' + s[end:]

+from pathlib import Path
+
+root = Path('tasks')
+
+def edit(name, changes=(), inserts=()):
+    path = root / name
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    for old, new in changes:
+        if old not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing {old[:80]!r}')
+        s = s.replace(old, new)
+    for marker, addition in inserts:
+        if marker not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing marker {marker!r}')
+        s = s.replace(marker, addition + '\n\n' + marker, 1)
+    path.write_text(s, encoding='utf-8')
+

+from pathlib import Path
+
+root = Path('tasks')
+
+def edit(name, changes=(), inserts=()):
+    path = root / name
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    for old, new in changes:
+        if old not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing {old[:80]!r}')
+        s = s.replace(old, new)
+    for marker, addition in inserts:
+        if marker not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing marker {marker!r}')
+        s = s.replace(marker, addition + '\n\n' + marker, 1)
+    path.write_text(s, encoding='utf-8')
+

+from pathlib import Path
+
+root = Path('tasks')
+
+def edit(name, changes=(), inserts=()):
+    path = root / name
+    s = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+    for old, new in changes:
+        if old not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing {old[:80]!r}')
+        s = s.replace(old, new)
+    for marker, addition in inserts:
+        if marker not in s:
+            raise ValueError(f'{name}: missing marker {marker!r}')
+        s = s.replace(marker, addition + '\n\n' + marker, 1)
+    path.write_text(s, encoding='utf-8')
+
